Here you go, Mr. Ercoshkun.
tbmm-2016-06-22sit01spe0002par0010	Thank you. Mr. President, we have won the term presidency as Turkish President of Turkey at the Union Parliamentary Assembly for the Mediterranean, which took place in Tanca, Morocco, on May 28th and 29th.
tbmm-2016-06-22sit01spe0003par0013	Bravo, bravo!
tbmm-2016-06-22sit01spe0002par0014	First of all, I wish that our term presidency would bring blessings to our country, to the Mediterranean basin, to the region during the European-Akdeniz meeting. Turkey was one of the founding members of the European-Akdeniz Parliamentary Forum, established in 2004 as the parliamentary dimension of the European-Akdeniz Process, also known as the Barcelona Process, and has been a member of the Union for the Mediterranean Organization for Barcelona, which has been operating as the continuation of the Barcelona Process since 2008. In short, there are 280 parliamentary members from 43 countries, including 28 member states and 15 countries on the coast of the Mediterranean. The group I chaired includes seven members, Mr. Fevzi Shanverdi, Aksaray MP Mustafa Serdensetti, Bursa MP Ceyhun İrgil, Mersin MP Mr. Serdal Welcuoğlu and Diyarbakır MP Altan Tan. In short, there are five separate committees, including the Political Committee, the Economic and Financial Affairs Committee, the Cultural Committee, the Energy Committee and the Women's Rights Committee. At the General Assembly following the two annual meetings of each committee, reports prepared are presented to parliamentary members' opinions, and reports being drawn are approved by the General Assembly. At the 2012 General Assembly, I was elected President of the Economic Committee in the 24th term, and I have been the President of the Economic Committee ever since. As the Economic Committee in various places in Turkey, our meetings, including Istanbul, Ankara, Bolu, Antalya, especially the immigration and refugee issue in the region, the inequality between the European coast and the southern countries, especially the injustices, the double standards -- the various activities we've made, the various reports we've prepared. In fact, after these reports, many of our proposals came to the attention, especially after the Arab Spring, at the point of the migration and refugee raid over the region to solve the problems it has created on both Europe and the countries in the region. Of course, the developments in Syria, the developments in Iraq, the problems of Libya and countries in the region concerning both immigration and refugee problems, as well as the problems of terrorism over the entire region, are among the main issues of the assembly. In light of our recent work as President of the Economic Committee, about six months ago, we presented our view of the term presidency to Mr. Speaker, and, as our Mr. President found positive, we were nominated as President of Turkey at this time. These elections, which coincided with May 28th-29th, have unfortunately coincided with the dates of the vote of confidence in our government. To be honest, even though we weren't here ourselves, our hearts were there. That's how I would like to express our lack of confidence in our newly established government. With the spirit of a national team, I would like to say once again that Turkey has been elected from all countries to the presidency of the term, with the support of the General Assembly, including our fellow MP Ceyhun İrgil, and our other fellow MPs. With this election for four years, we'll be running the term president for one year and the term vice president for three years. Mr. Speaker Ismail Kahraman wishes that these activities under the leadership of the General Assembly will contribute to the stability of the region, to the presence of the Mediterranean, to the creation of a peace lake again, and I respectfully salute the General Assembly.

tbmm-2016-06-22sit01spe0005par0028	Mr. President, I've been promised off the agenda on the problems of the forest workers. On this occasion, I salute you all. The Forest General Directorate includes about 8,500 temporary workers, as well as permanent workers, who have been employed by Law 5620 for years. Workers who work five months and 29 days a year lack job security and cannot look to the future with confidence. Do you ever wonder how temporary workers can make a living in months when they can't work? These friends of ours are also suffering from retirement problems. It's very difficult for them to fill the bonus days. Because it takes more than 40 years of hard work to retire five months and twenty-nine days a year. The strangeness and contradiction is that when the forest administration doesn't employ temporary workers, it employs thousands of subcontractors that it employs through service, and tens of thousands of workers are employed in community benefit programs. In the case of workers employed for five months and twenty-nine days, there will be no need for a year's work, no need for a tender for service, and on the other hand, there will be increased efficiency in their workplaces because the staff issue will be resolved. With that respect, the arrangement that has been successful over the years, with its knowledge, skill, and experience, should be made to ensure that temporary and seasonal workers who are useful to the workplace can work continuously. On May 30, 2015, during the signing of the Public Community Business Contracts Framework Agreement Protocol, the present-day Prime Minister is working for five months and twenty-nine days, taking a break. This affects business productivity and efficiency negatively. He foretold that temporary workers' work limits would be lifted. Article 9 of the protocol was also ruled for this purpose. Minister of Labour Faruk Çelik of that day, we have registered the arrangement for the execution of these workers in a collective agreement with Parliament. It will be passed quickly. He said so. Look at the speed of the AKP government, it's been over a year, but there's nothing. The promises have not been kept, and they have all failed. It is clear that the AKP government has deceived temporary and seasonal workers. In addition to forest workers, our temporary and seasonal workers who work in railways, agricultural businesses, sugar factories, tea factories and other public administrations are looking for us and saying they are disappointed by the government. The AKP has also deceived subcontractors. The AKP's announcements of good news to subcontractors ahead of the election have been announced by the secondary media and have been stamped and presented to the public. And after the election, the word of the cast became a position, but until today, it's not been given, and there's still nothing. The AKP's government is more interested in ignoring judicial decisions and taking away their rights than in giving rights to subcontractors. I'm calling from here to the government: let's give the subcontractors their rights, as well as the temporary and seasonal workers. Let's just make sure they're assigned to the regular staff right now. Let's get rid of this friend of ours. We, the National Movement Party, are in, and we call it a challenge. Dear lawmakers, a major problem with forest workers is about overtime. Workers who work 24 hours a day during the fire season are unable to get their rights. Workers suing for overtime rights are threatened and appointed. Workers must not be wronged, they must be allowed to receive their rights for overtime. In addition, the overtime exercise must be cut off, and 12 months of overtime must be calculated, considering that there may be fires in our country at all times. Again, forest administration has anti-employment practices that prevent workers from becoming members of the union they want. This tyranny must be stopped, the constitutional rights of workers must not be blocked. I thank you, I pay my respects.

With all these features, sports determines the measure of socioeconomic development in countries. The contribution of a country to sports and the development of its athletes also determines the level of development and development of that country. Although sports may seem like individual pursuits, it is essentially an event that requires very serious infrastructure work, planned and programmed preparation. In our country, sports activities in different fields and branches are conducted by amateurs and professional clubs, as well as individual pursuits. Our amateur sports clubs, which conduct activities in different counties, also vary in terms of condition and resource efficiency. This makes it difficult to conduct the event with the problems created by unfair conditions. The lack of basic tools, such as facilities, materials, equipment, is at the top of basic shortcomings for amateur sports clubs to perform their activities. The Ministry of Youth and Sports, whose mission and purpose is to "determinate policies that support young people's personal and social development," lacks assistance and investment, and amateur sports clubs struggle to survive. Many amateur clubs continue to exist with the support of municipalities, tradesmen and industrialists due to financial inadequacies. Due to financial impotence and lack of facilities, many amateur clubs have to prepare for competitions and fight at other clubs' facilities. Club administrations also have to deal with another problem due to tax and insurance premium payments. It's a problem for clubs not to be able to find a manager. In all this trouble, clubs are struggling to afford transportation costs to go to training and competitions. In particular, it is important for individual and community health and development to support amateur sports activities that strengthen young people's participation in social life, which perform an important event in saving young people from coffee corners and criminally inclined behavior disorders. The protection of our young people's body and soul health should be supported by amateur sports clubs for the foundation of a healthy society, and more amateur clubs in the provinces and districts should be allowed to provide sports opportunities for our young people. In addition to social integration, it will also facilitate the fight for civility and prevention of bad habits such as drugs. For all these reasons, it is vital that the problems of amateur sports clubs be investigated and determined, the solutions to this issue, the setting of legislation and legal regulations, the solution to the problem by the Supreme Assembly, the discussion of its individual and social development and the context of a national issue.
